#   ## THE OPENER: Tony's ruling on defineCapture's shape (objectModelRecon §27). One plain line: a define whose name already

#   * Attribute traits MUST keep writing through: operateMethod= ruleMethod= jitEmitter= parseAction= file= code= are commands
#     receiving their argument by having the value written into their own entry.
#   * Own names resolving outside (table in §27b): with a value at the trait -> all accidental (counter, 13 DesignDocs entries,
#     cursorReadTb taG, KantGrammar loopOn*); bare with nothing after -> all intended (every content reference of recon 19, still
#     bare); bare with members/attrs after -> mixed: WardeD (a rule) extended on purpose, Decoder canonOf / TOKENize, McCap maximus,
#     Utilities parser captured; KantGrammar's registry and Modifiers' repeatClass references intended.
#   * Value capture (aCTionTraiTdata promoting a plain field): 0 across the fleet; the fixit's tdV=maximus+ is the only case.

# ⚠⚠⚠ SEALED 2026-10-29 (251) ON TRUNK -- STOP (SEQ 453, the dispatch's own STOP clause). THE OPTIONAL-FLAGS CAUSE IS GENERAL, NOT FormaT'S. NOTHING BUILT.
#
#   ## THE OPENER: Tony's word on the cure's SCOPE. One plain line: a format with no flags fails under the generated
#   parser because any set term written with a repeat after its brackets (`[...]+`, `*`, `?`) is stored as a group, and the
#   generator installs no parse method for a group; the term then runs the old parse, which answers a miss with failure and
#   never reads the term's `?`. Two terms under parser(Start) are hit (FormaT's flags, Precision's precision); one more is
#   latent (tell's message). Nothing is fixed yet; the cure needs a ruling on where it goes.
#
#   ## STEP 1 (read only): the two spellings are identical but for the rule names --
#     incant/kantGrammar:40   FORMAt          pound="#" flags?=[-# 0+']+ formatWIDTH?=NumbeR PRECISIOn? formatTYPE=[a-zA-Z] TOKENize;
#     incant/grammar:122      FormaT          pound="#" flags?=[-# 0+']+ formatWIDTH?=NumbeR Precision? formatTYPE=[a-zA-Z] TOKENize;
#   (and kantGrammar:38 PRECISIOn / grammar:121 Precision, `dot=[.] precision?=[0-9]+`, likewise). printFormatT never calls
#   parser(), so FORMAt works there on the interpreted walk. The difference is the ROAD, not the spelling.
#   ## STEP 2 (read only; probes in the scratchpad, lldb on the live binary, no repo file touched):
#   * Generated body (baselineTestsNew stdout): `FormaT = CodE { return pound() && flags() && formatWIDTH() && Precision() &&
#     formatTYPE(); }`. flags, pound, dot, precision, formatTYPE carry data, so they get no body; formatWIDTH (`return
#     NumbeR();`) and Precision (`return dot() && precision();`) do.
#   * traceParse() + measureParseClass over parser(Start): flags method=none, precision method=none; dot, formatTYPE =
#     parseSet; formatWIDTH, Precision = parseRule. 124 faces, 4 none: flags, precision, ShortcuT, dtext.
#   * Data kinds (bare reads in an iterate walk): flags 6 (isGROUP), precision 6, formatTYPE 3 (isSET), dot 3. One variable at
#     a time on scratch rules: `a=[0-9]+` 6, `d=[0-9]*` 6, `h=[0-9]?` 6, `GpS=[0-9]+;` (a rule that is only a set) 6;
#     `c?=[0-9]` 3, `i=[0-9]` 3. So a modifier AFTER the brackets makes the term a GROUP; `?=` on the label does not.
#     Exceptions: the bootstrap's numberSet / power / decimals and grammar:55 NotA are isSET (NotA sits inside DatA's own
#     block, before DatA closes -- that ordering as the cause is NOT measured). ANYstring (grammar:114, near-identical set) is 6.
#   * setParseWalk (Generate.rtn:474) `case isGROUP: field.parseMethod = null;` -- no method, no hasNewParse.
#   * lldb, driveStep conditioned on rule tag flags, under parser(Start) on formatTest: opCall -> runRule -> driveStep with
#     the mark at `12s maybe:; }`; with no hasNewParse driveStep takes the old-road arm `rule->parse(...)`
#     (GroupRules.mm:2510), which returns `labelNO`; runAccAnd reads it false and the && chain fails. formatWIDTH instead runs
#     parseRule -> exitFromParse, whose minZeroIsSatisfied (Generate.rtn:105) turns a min-0 miss into trueResult. Nothing on
#     the old-road arm reads min.
#   * NOT re-measured: the seal-249 spellings flags?=FormatFlags / flags=FormatFlags? (a named set rule is also isGROUP, but
#     why flags itself then never reached exitFromParse is not shown here).
#   ## THE STOP: the cause is the generator's handling of a repeated set term (isGROUP -> no method), so it reaches every such
#   term. CENSUS (every `[..]` + modifier in incant/grammar, kantGrammar, setup, utilities, unitTests, the parser, tester and
#   every incant/pop fixture): BITES under parser(Start) -- optional, no method: grammar:122 flags, grammar:121 precision.
#   REACHED, no method, mandatory, works on the old road: grammar:112 ShortcuT, grammar:50 dtext (`dtext^=delimiter}`, an
#   upTo, not a set). LATENT -- optional, not under Start: utilities:11 tell `message?=[^\n]+` (and compileInT:69's copy),
#   if parser() ever reaches it. Mandatory repeated sets elsewhere (HeX value, ANYstring, leafLabelT LlWord) run the old road
#   and match -- leafLabelT's header already records (2026-10-05) that inline sets do not take the new-road leaf path.
#   Two cure shapes, neither built: (a) setParseWalk installs parseSet (or a new arm) for a group that holds a set, so the
#   term reaches exitFromParse; (b) the old-road arm in driveStep honours a min-0 term's miss as success. (a) moves every
#   repeated set term onto the new road (parseClass.target and the PARSECLASS counts would move); (b) touches only optional
#   terms. Tony's ruling.

# ⚠⚠⚠ SEALED 2026-10-17 (168) ON TRUNK -- SEQ 375 STOPPED AT THE RECON: JRS-1's CURE REACHES THE JITTED FRAME MODEL. NOTHING BUILT.
#
#   ## THE OPENER: a ruling on how a jitted function holds its argument. What was run: one tap in the clone (followArgument
#   under jitting, runAction's jitting arm, jitBindArgRT at run time, jitSeedField / jitSeedLiteral; env-gated, off =
#   base byte-identical, reverted md5-identical) on jitRecurSaveT with the IR dump.
#   THREE THINGS READ WRONG, measured:
#   (a) SHARED NODE -- YES, the jit twin of SEQ 374, one level over. Every `argument` read in rsF follows the binding at
#   EMIT time to the same node: the literal 4 rsDrive passed (0x...95a0, isLiteral). It is seeded once (jitSeedLiteral,
#   constant 4), and each compare's result rides back on its target's jitData (jitEmitCompare, jitEmitters.rtn:1195). So
#   `argument < 0` emits i1 false and leaves it on the node; `argument == 0` then compares THAT with 0 -> br i1 true;
#   `t = argument` stores i1 true. rsG's `add i32 %frame, i1 false` (R2's invalid IR) is the same mechanism.
#   (b) THE ARGUMENT IS NEVER A RUN-TIME VALUE. jitEmitSelfCall bakes the emit-time argument node into bindArg and the
#   call: both rsDrive's rsF(4) and rsF's rsF(argument - 1) pass the literal-4 node; `argument - 1` folds to a constant
#   nobody reads (no sub in the IR). At run time jit_rsF is entered once (one RT bind), takes the folded `== 0` branch and
#   returns.
#   (c) THE CALL'S VALUE IS DROPPED. runAction returns `field` for an emitted self-call, so `r = rsF(...)` and
#   `rs1 = rsF(4)` seed the ACTION node rsF and load its gCount; %selfcall is never read. rs1 ends isTOKEN (datA 14), empty.
#   WHY STOP: (c) is local to the self-call; (a) and (b) are not. The argument must be a per-call RUN-TIME value inside a
#   separately built function (read through the binding at run time, or passed in -- the function's unused `ptr %field`
#   parameter, or a frame slot), and an argument read must not be a node results ride on. That is the jitted frame
#   model, and it changes every jitted argument read, inlined callees included (they bind only at emit time). Options for
#   Tony: (1) the argument becomes a frame slot of the jitted function, stored from the call's computed value
#   (jitEmitSelfCall passes the i32; the prologue stores it); (2) the argument is read at run time through ruleArg's
#   binding, with a run-time bind on every call (inlined ones too); (3) rule (c) alone now as a self-call fix, knowing
#   JRS-1 stays wrong until (1) or (2).

#
#   ## THE OPENER: a ruling on R1's spelling. R0 (jit matches interpreted, a refusal ends the body) stands. But most
#   emit-time refusals are JIT-ONLY -- capability gaps the interpreted road never raises: acC "a call whose target is
#   built -- not yet on the jit road", `:. tempField` (jitSetFlagTemp), the op= fold step on a copy-landing shape. Today
#   they DEGRADE: the outer emitter gets a null, the whole statement runs interpreted at run time (jitStmtRT, which clears
#   the refusal either side), so jit == interpreted. An emitted raise-and-exit at every such statement (R1 literally)
#   makes them diverge: jitBlockCtlT jit pz 7 -> 0 (interpreted 7), jitXscRT's fire rows, jitOpEqFoldT JOF-4 (interpreted
#   a 10 r 15). Recommended spelling, which keeps R0 and "by reach": at a statement whose emit refused, emit ONE run-time
#   call that runs the statement on the interpreted road (jitStmtRT's shape) and, if THAT run refused, leaves the
#   function (runAction's exit; inside an inlined callee, its exitBB). A language refusal then raises by name at run time
#   exactly as interpreted; a jit-only one does not; an untaken branch runs nothing. Two consequences to rule with it:
#   (1) `a :. noPrinT` would then SET the flag at run time -- jitSetFlagTemp's OTHER half closes too, against R3's
#   "stays open"; (2) jitValueTargetT's one body would end at its first refusal on both roads, so all eleven value rows
#   (JVK / JR2 / JVP controls included) lose their subject (interpreted today: tag echoes jv1 ... jp1) -- the fixture
#   needs splitting, one action per case, to keep its controls.
#
#   ## CENSUS: tap in the clone (refuse() under jitting, stderr, env-gated; removed md5-identical, rebuilt), every fixture
#   pop.sh / ladder.sh resolve (incant, incant/pop, incant/pop/jit, incant/fixits): jitValueTargetT 4 (refuseValueTarget,
#   a LANGUAGE refusal -- interpreted raises it too), jitXscRT 2 and jitBlockCtlT 1 (acC, jit-only), jitOpEqFoldT 1 (the
#   fold step, jit-only for that shape), fixit jitSetFlagTemp 1 (`:. tempField`, jit-only). Twins: jitValueTargetT
#   interpreted ends at its first refusal (every row a tag echo); jitOpEqFoldT interpreted 10 5 / ab cd / joQ / 10 15;
#   jitBlockCtlT interpreted pz 7; jitXscRT interpreted 0 0 / 0 1 (= jitted). Body measures: `-n += 3; b.noPrinT = 1;
#   c = 7; ++d` interpreted b 0 c 1 d 5, jitted b 1 c 7 d 6; `a :. noPrinT; ...` interpreted (no refusal) b 1 c 7 d 6,
#   jitted b 0 c 7 d 5. No interpreted row would move under either spelling.

#   ## THE OPENER: a ruling on R0's premise. Measured on the interpreted road: an action body whose statement 1 refuses
#   (-a += 3) does NOT run statements 2-4 (b.noPrinT stays 0, c 1, d 5). aCTionBlocK breaks on ruler.refused
#   ("A REFUSAL STOPS THE BLOCK", interpreted only) and runAction clears it at the activation exit ("a refusal ends the
#   action that raised it and NOT its caller"). The 09-17 ruling (docs/refusalScope.md) is the PARSE-LEVEL statement

#   -- would make the jit road run statements the interpreted road skips. Either (a) the jit road matches the

#
#   ## THE OPENER: Tony rules the cure's scope (expressionRecon "jitDivide"). The census says `/` cannot be cured alone to R1:
#   jitted assignment KEEPS the target's kind and stores the raw result (a double into a count reads 0; an integer into a

#   - **The yield channel (F-122 entry 7):** a construct hands its parent a VALUE; the label tree never holds a field,
#     a property node or a stand-in label; an unrun IF hands back nothing. **break / continue / return are CONTROL,
#     on a channel of their own** (the keyword action writes it, the firing parent reads it after each child).
#     Parse-deciding nulls stay exempt. All of it LANDS WITH STEP 2 -- nothing changed now.
#   - **A rejected parse fires nothing** (ruling (ii)): actions fire from a tree; the rejection is the report. The
#     exempt class stays only code that DECIDES the parse -- a diagnostic does not qualify.
#   - **The recording scope** (ruling A): a drive entered while firing is its own root. One fact (gPtfScope), one
#     writer pair, called from the two drive seats (driveStep, jitProbeDrive). The old road pushes nothing.
#   - **The "g"+tag stamp retired** (ruling C): zero live readers.

#     overwrites that attribute (bear-trap #56).
